down%2C+eyes+never+leaving+the+same+point.+No+word+is+spoken%2C+the+scene+stays+wordless%3B+and+when+the+shadow+crosses+his+face+he+does+NOT+turn+toward+it%3A+his+eyes+stay+pinned+to+that+same+spot+while+the+darkness+swallows+his+rim+light.+BACKGROUND%3A+the+threat+assembles)

* * *

### Scene 3 — The Train

**What it does:** Animates the 16-shot western train robbery, with three hand-drawn storyboards fixing the spatial logic.

**Assets:** @Nate\_train, @Aila\_train, @train, @storyboard1, @storyboard2, @storyboard3

![8a3e22d8-6707-4568-88ab-8e9a873d8f79.png](https://d2ol7oe51mr4n9.cloudfront.net/user_3G85t64kByVQ9PWe1mWsRtmS5UX/e8ac308c-1a67-4a99-9192-1f6e41ffef5c.png)

![3a02e944-bd83-44fb-8666-eafd72b3241a.png](https://d2ol7oe51mr4n9.cloudfront.net/user_3G85t64kByVQ9PWe1mWsRtmS5UX/22b9985e-3702-4a13-985e-107833e685e7.png)

![hf_20260801_012538_d98665d3-3615-4b29-9565-ab8cb07e3ebf.png](https://d2ol7oe51mr4n9.cloudfront.net/user_3G85t64kByVQ9PWe1mWsRtmS5UX/3e7856a8-cfd7-44e4-94f5-d38c7af88a8f.png)

![6165205a-01bf-4abe-92c6-c21017f183b3.png](https://d2ol7oe51mr4n9.cloudfront.net/user_3G85t64kByVQ9PWe1mWsRtmS5UX/4a730665-34a6-4c65-951f-99c73dcdd4ca.png)

![image_2026-08-20_02-26-09 (1).png](https://d2ol7oe51mr4n9.cloudfront.net/user_3G85t64kByVQ9PWe1mWsRtmS5UX/36d5ba9e-c155-4bb9-9213-3da67b441940.png)

![4c22d805-fa6b-47e0-a4a0-e67cf009acba.png](https://d2ol7oe51mr4n9.cloudfront.net/user_3G85t64kByVQ9PWe1mWsRtmS5UX/69b5b6ad-4849-43de-94ff-c9b3306fff21.png)

SCENE CONTEXT

A 30-second, 16-shot western train-robbery action sequence, opening IN MEDIAS RES — mid-chase, mid-firefight, no establishing shot: a cliff-side railway, then a tall stone viaduct over a sunlit sea. CLEAR SUNNY DAY, hard direct sunlight in every shot. The heroine — confident, playful — leads the masked pack and leaps onto a MIDDLE carriage before the road ends; the hero — a simple TRYING man: light doubt, a touch of honest fear, a slightly untidy manner, never comedic — trails at the tail on a horse fractionally SLOWER than the train, is overtaken, stands on his galloping horse, leaps, nearly misses, climbs up; on the roofs she drops a guard, throws him one farewell smile and dives into the sea; he wins his own fight barely, sees only her vanishing — and the train carries him into a tunnel; darkness swallows the frame. No dialogue (one short yelp in shot 1). 30.0 s total.

ACTIVE REFERENCES

@c00a8d57-18ee-4e07-ab45-10e1d4686ad1 — cliff-side railway, reference looks FORWARD along the train: grey rock wall on the train's left, dark-red carriages with pale rounded roofs, dirt road / golden slope / sea drop on the right, stone viaduct and tunnel headland ahead. Terrain, train and geometry only; the reference's own camera is never inherited; weather locked to hard sun.

@d2b455f0-0f1e-4e8c-877b-e01ad54803de — hero: dark cowboy hat, brown leather coat with shearling collar, navy neckerchief, face clean, uncovered. 100% match; ignore studio background. Brown horse.

@86cef2a7-9de0-491b-8830-5d03fbd0c1d7 — heroine: long dark wavy hair; pale sand wide-brim felt hat; wine-red neck scarf (over her nose at the start of shot 3, pulled down within it, down afterward); cream linen shirt; pale beige worn canvas duster, open; brown leather gun belt; sand-tan fringed trousers in tall dark-brown boots — sun-bleached 1880s trail wear. 100% match; ignore studio background. Light palomino horse.

@08cb9833-6fe6-4724-8a37-bc25e349d2d1— storyboard sketch for SHOT 1: frontal chest-up, hero large at center landing onto his horse; frame-right the LAST carriage's rear end cuts in, track receding; a gang rider in the defocused depth. Composition/framing/blocking only, not drawing style.

@b6bf6371-368e-4dcb-a6c3-38e1e54c6013 — storyboard sketch for SHOT 2: side view, hero standing balanced on his galloping horse at center, arms out; frame-right the LAST car, rear end facing frame-left, empty track behind; all movement screen-right. Composition/screen direction only. CORRECTIONS: the standing pose is the shot's MID-POINT — the first frame has him SEATED, rising on camera.

@1fc1c95d-49fb-4c5b-8d68-932a0574d7c3 — storyboard sketch for SHOT 3 (reverse axis): hero mid-leap at lower frame-left flying left-to-right toward the carriage at frame-right; riders behind his horse. Composition/screen direction only. CORRECTION: frame-right shows ONLY the last car's rear edge and rear platform — no further carriages; he leaps from the track behind the train onto its very tail.

GEOGRAPHY AND GLOBAL LOCKS

THE WORLD MUST READ WITHOUT AN ESTABLISHING SHOT — the full geography lives inside every frame: a long dark-red train charging at full steam along a cliff-side railway, grey rock wall hard on the train's LEFT, a dirt road with the masked rider gang on its RIGHT, golden slopes falling to a sunlit sea beyond the road; the robbery is ALREADY in full swing from the very first frame — riders and train defenders trading fire, dust, steam and muzzle flashes establishing the chase inside the action itself. The road ends at the viaduct. Six riders as one pack along the rear half of the train: the heroine beside the MIDDLE carriages, the hero the REARMOST, level with the LAST car for the whole ground section. RIDER ESCORT LOCK: in every ground shot with the HERO on horseback, TWO masked gang riders gallop BEHIND him, trading fire with the train; in every shot with the HEROINE on horseback, TWO masked gang riders gallop AHEAD of her, trading fire with the train; and in every backward-looking shot (including the climb in shot 8) TWO riders read in the receding background, firing at the carriages. Defenders fire sideways; rider fire angles left into the train, never at the lens. SPEED LOCK: train and horses at full speed always; only the hero's horse is slower — the train drifts forward relative to him in every ground shot: level in 1, half a car behind in 3, fully overtaken in 5. Empty horses: hers veers up the slope; his keeps chasing on the track, slowly falling back. ROOF MAP: she is on the THIRD carriage from the locomotive, he fights on the FOURTH; the roofs behind him are completely EMPTY. Exactly two anonymous guards: A — grey railway waistcoat, rolled sleeves, grey flat cap, revolver NEVER leaves its holster; B — long brown coat, brown bowler. Roof section has NO gunfire, all hand-to-hand; both guards end unconscious ON the roof. Backward-axis shots (1, 3, 5, 8): the receding background holds ONLY track, rock wall, road, slope, sea — NO viaduct, bridge, tunnel or arches. Hats never fly off, including her dive. She looks back exactly once (shot 13); the dive is off the train's RIGHT side only. No insects.

LIGHTING LOCK

CLEAR SUNNY DAY: high hard sun, deep blue cloudless sky, hard sun modeling every face and surface — crisp deep shadows, golden bounce, hard sea glints, bright steam; consistent sun direction; slow motion equally bright. No clouds, haze or dusk; no lens flares.

FORMAT

Seventeen hard cuts, 30.0 s total; only shot 7 is slow motion. Widescreen.

GLOBAL OPTICS

Extreme anamorphic glass: strongly oval vertical bokeh, subtle squeeze, flare-suppressed.

SHOT 1 — 0.0-2.8s — MATCH-CUT LANDING, frontal tracking medium close-up, reverse axis (rock wall frame-RIGHT), 47° — composition per @08cb9833-6fe6-4724-8a37-bc25e349d2d1. The scene OPENS mid-action: full gallop beside the speeding train's LAST car, dust flying, gunfire cracking — and he drops vertically into frame, slamming into the saddle — a short involuntary yelp, hand grabbing the horn, an earnest untidy recovery, coat askew, hat knocked crooked; in the same breath he FIRES once diagonally forward into the train's flank (never at the lens); an answering shot cracks back and he ducks low over the neck, honestly flinching — a beat of light real fear — then his eyes settle forward; BEHIND him in the depth TWO masked riders gallop, trading fire with the train. Diligent, not smooth, never comedic.

SHOT 2 — 2.8-4.8s — HER 3/4 REAR TRACKING: THE SHOT AND THE SMIRK, forward axis (rock wall frame-LEFT), 63°. Camera behind her right shoulder, sea side, at her speed: @86cef2a7-9de0-491b-8830-5d03fbd0c1d7 beside the middle carriages, back, hat and streaming hair dominating, scarf up over her nose; the train runs frame-left into depth, defender flashes in the bokeh; AHEAD of her, deeper along the road, TWO masked riders gallop trading fire with the forward carriages; deep behind, the hero's small figure at the tail. She levels her revolver left and fires once — a rifleman's silhouette on a roof drops in the blur; without breaking rhythm she pulls the scarf DOWN off her face, turns her head back over her RIGHT shoulder — almost into the lens, sun in her eyes — one slow, flirty, knowing smirk past camera at him far behind. Then forward again: she has seen where the road ends.

SHOT 3 — 4.8-6.0s — HIS 3/4 CLOSE-UP, reverse axis, 47°. His sunlit face at full gallop, hat brim trembling; behind, the last car drifts slowly forward against the rock wall frame-right — the train faster; revolver half-lowered in the lower frame, not aiming; in the defocused depth behind him TWO masked riders gallop, firing at the train. Eyes locked dead AHEAD on her line — never sideways, never back: NO anger — a LIGHT PUZZLEMENT, brows faintly raised-and-knit, a man not quite understanding how the gap keeps growing; hopeful more than confident, one flicker of doubt chased off by trying; a soft sun-squint, not a glare.

SHOT 4 — 6.0-8.0s — OVER-THE-SHOULDER WIDE: HER JUMP AT THE EDGE, 84°. Camera behind the hero's shoulder at the very rear: his shoulder and hat anchor frame-right, the last car frame-left, the train stretching to the locomotive; four riders mid-depth trading fire — flashes both ways, splinters, sunlit dust; ahead the road visibly tapers to its end at the viaduct and the drop; at the pack's head she angles her palomino tight against a MIDDLE carriage. He can only watch: meters from the drop she rises to STAND on her saddle at full gallop, rides one balanced beat, and leaps right-to-left at the last possible moment — catching the middle carriage's door rail, boots on the step, climbing straight for the roof, clean, no hesitation; her empty horse veers hard up the sunlit slope — while the tail keeps inching away from him.

SHOT 5 — 8.0-9.4s — HIS FRONTAL CLOSE-UP: OVERTAKEN, THE DECISION, reverse axis, 47°. The last car's sunlit side slides visibly forward past him — its rear edge sweeps out of frame-right, leaving empty rails and open road: the train has outrun him; TWO masked riders hold behind him in the depth, still trading fire. One disbelieving glance at the departing tail, then up at the bright sky — playing SILENTLY: jaw tightening, a sharp exhale through the nose, lips pressed, not a word — and in the same breath understanding turns into resolve with a short swallow of doubt inside it: revolver holstered in one practical motion, both hands on the horn, he starts pushing himself up. Cut mid-rise.

SHOT 6 — 9.4-11.4s — SIDE TRACKING: THE RISE AND PUSH-OFF, 84° — per corrected @b6bf6371-368e-4dcb-a6c3-38e1e54c6013. From the sea-side road, the backdrop entirely the sunlit ROCK WALL (sea behind camera, never in frame): his horse pounds the track directly behind the last car's empty rear platform. Beats: one boot onto the saddle, then the other — rising shakily to fully standing, one boot slipping once, coat askew, arms out; soft knees, a quick swallow, one flicker of doubt at the rushing ground, eyes back to the car; a last half-second hesitation, a crouch — and he DRIVES forward off the saddle. Cut as his boots leave it.

SHOT 7 — 11.4-13.2s — SLOW-MOTION FLIGHT, low angle 45° up, 63° — per corrected @1fc1c95d-49fb-4c5b-8d68-932a0574d7c3. The scene's only slow motion: camera very low on the sea side of the rail bed, tilted up: he sails through the upper frame against the deep blue sky, body stretched long, arms reaching frame-right for the last car's empty rear, coat wide open, neckerchief flaring, hat on; sun and golden track-bed bounce keep his face fully readable — light honest fear and doubt in the eyes; lower frame-left his horse's chest and forelegs, saddle empty. Cut BEFORE his hands reach the roof edge — real time snaps back with the cut.

SHOT 8 — 13.2-16.8s — CAMERA ON THE LAST CAR'S REAR EDGE: THE MISS, THE PAUSE, THE CLIMB, reverse axis (rock wall frame-RIGHT), 63°. The camera sits on the roof at its very rear edge, very low, looking straight back: the bare roof edge runs across the lower frame centimeters from the lens; track recedes up center, rock wall frame-right, sea frame-left — no bridge or tunnel anywhere. Beats: (1) he flies AT the camera, growing, reaching — and falls SHORT: his hands never touch the edge, no grab, no slip — he simply drops out of the bottom of frame; the empty horse gallops at camera, slowly losing ground, and deeper in the sunlit dust TWO masked riders gallop, trading fire with the train. (2) a LONG suspended pause, over two seconds — empty edge, receding track, the horse and the two riders in the dust, wind — did he make it? held honestly long enough to truly doubt him. (3) a hand slaps up over the edge close to the lens, a second lands beside it; his face rises LARGE into the lower frame, hat on, wind-pressed, simple honest relief — he pulls up only to CHEST height, forearms on the roof, stops, and lifts his eyes FORWARD toward her far up the train. Cut on that look.

SHOT 9 — 16.8-18.6s — HER RUN AND THE STOPPED DRAW, REAR THREE-QUARTER from the RIGHT, 84°. Camera off the train's right over the sea, level with the roofs, BEHIND her and outboard — a rear 3/4 view tracking with her: her figure from behind-and-beside, hair and duster whipping, running full stride along the THIRD carriage roof into the depth; at the gap ahead GUARD A — grey waistcoat, rolled sleeves, flat cap — hauls up onto the roof, hand going for his revolver; before it clears leather she reaches him, pins the draw arm, and a fast compact hand-to-hand fight erupts on the swaying roof. Cut mid-exchange.

SHOT 10 — 18.6-19.8s — HIS REACTION, backward axis, 47°. Frontal tracking close-up: he runs toward camera along the FOURTH roof, the roofs behind him completely EMPTY — only bare receding roofline and sea both sides. Mid-stride his eyes lock past camera on her fight — rhythm stumbles half a step, breath catches: fear, for the first time, aimed at someone else; he pushes harder. Cut on the acceleration.

SHOT 11 — 19.8-21.4s — HIS OWN AMBUSH, forward axis, 63°. Camera behind him at roof level: his running back centered on the fourth roof; at the gap GUARD B — brown coat, bowler — climbs up and seizes his lapels: a graceless desperate grapple, the hero bent back over the roof edge, boots sliding, clawing for hold; mid-struggle he steals one wild glance toward her — and nearly pays for it. Cut inside the struggle.

SHOT 12 — 21.4-23.2s — SHE FINISHES IT, WALKS TO THE EDGE, SLIGHTLY LATERAL angle, 63°. Camera at roof level beside-and-behind her fight — a light side offset, not a straight rear view: GUARD A lunges with everything; she sidesteps, hooks the arm, uses his own momentum — one clean economical throw, and he crumples flat, out cold, dead weight rocking with the sway. She doesn't even straighten her hat. Unhurried, she walks diagonally to the roof's RIGHT edge and stops at the brink, wind pulling her duster, the sea far below. Cut as she stops.

SHOT 13 — 23.2-25.2s — HER FAREWELL SMILE, close-up, slow push-in, 47°. From behind and outboard of her right shoulder at the third roof's right edge: hat, streaming hair and shoulder dominate, sea and arch defocused in oval bokeh; as the camera creeps in she turns her head back over her RIGHT shoulder — almost into the lens, sun catching her eyes — finds him one carriage back. One LIGHT, thin, FLIRTY farewell smile past camera — playful, teasing, barely-there — held a beat at the tightest point; her only look back. Then she turns away to the sea. Cut on the turn.

SHOT 14 — 25.2-27.0s — HIS KO, HER JUMP IN THE BACKGROUND, forward axis, 63°. Behind his grapple: he and GUARD B torque at center; in the bright depth one roof ahead, small at frame-right, she stands at the right edge. Half-strangled, he winds up one wild graceless haymaker — it CONNECTS; B drops like a sack, out cold. In those same seconds her small figure steps off the edge and drops below the rooflines. He straightens panting — head snapping toward where she vanished. Cut on the snap.

SHOT 15 — 27.0-28.6s — AERIAL WIDE: THE DIVE AND THE TUNNEL, 84°. High off the train's right over the sea, tracking: the full dark-red train with bright steam crosses the viaduct left-to-right; at frame-right the locomotive calmly enters the ordinary tunnel portal in the headland; she commits into a clean confident dive — one hand pressing her hat, duster and hair streaming — a long accelerating fall past the grand arch; a small hard white splash blooms; on the third roof's right edge his tiny figure stays planted, head tracking her all the way down as carriages feed into the tunnel. Cut on the splash.

SHOT 16 — 28.6-30.0s — HIS LOSS AND THE DARK, close-up FROM BEHIND, forward axis, 47°. Roof-level tracking close-up framed FROM BEHIND HIM: his BACK to camera large in the foreground, body angled a touch off-axis — and his head turned BACK over his RIGHT shoulder toward the camera, so his FACE reads clearly in 3/4 past that right shoulder; chin dropped, gaze aimed steeply DOWN past the tail — well below the horizon — locked on the fading white splash ring; chest heaving; ahead of him, deeper in the frame, the dark tunnel mouth closes in. Mild surprise sinks into the feeling of a MISSED CHANCE — hope visibly draining, shoulders easing down, self-directed disappointment plain on his face; he doesn't move, doesn't duck, nothing touches him — the train simply carries him into the tunnel: darkness slides over and swallows the frame. Hard cut to black inside the tunnel roar. End.

ACTING

Scene event (unspoken): a dare that cannot be refused, closed by a goodbye nobody names. HERO — a trying man, not a cool one: diligent, a little untidy, small honest doubts and a light fear he pushes through, never comedic; the overtake is the world saying no and his face quietly refusing it; the untidiness lives in his manner, never in the leap; the climb is stubbornness beating gravity; in shot 10 fear points outward for the first time and makes him faster; the haymaker astonishes him; 16 is not grief — the smaller ache of a missed chance, darkness arriving while he still looks. HEROINE — she performs the robbery for an audience of one: the shot, the unmasking, the smirk held a beat too long are aimed at him; after it she never checks — not checking is the flex; the roof fight is housework; the one look back is the only goodbye she will spend, turned away before an answer can form; the dive is the signature: no hesitation, hat pressed like tipping it. She never speaks. Guards play "hold the roof", dead weight when out; riders play "win the firefight", never watching the leads. (Gazes task-engaged, natural blink cadence, no frozen stares.)

PHYSICS / AUDIO / STYLE

Full-speed train and four-beat gallop; his drift relative to the train smooth and readable, never a jump; the rise to standing genuinely unstable; both leaps carry real launch, arc and cloth drag; the miss is physically true — he lands short on the rear platform below frame, then a muscle-real pull-up to chest height; roof fights fight the sway; the stopped draw is a real grip contest; the throw uses the attacker's momentum; her dive: real push-off, clean arc, long fall, one small hard splash. No blood. Audio: pack hooves, gunfire with cliff echo (ground only), wind; muffled slow-motion wash, roaring back on the cut; a muffled impact below frame at the miss, then only wind and wheels through the pause; hollow viaduct drumming; fight thuds and wordless effort — no dialogue or gunfire on the roofs; the whistling drop, one distant splash; the tunnel roar swallowing all to black. His voice only the yelp in shot 2; no music, no narration, no subtitles. Style: widescreen western action realism, 35mm film, Kodak 500T — fine grain, slightly muted tones, gently pulled-down exposure keeping the hard sunny contrast, 1880s detail; sharp faces, natural 180° shutter blur, no ghosting, duplicated limbs or flicker.

CONSTRAINTS

Exactly two named characters, two guards (A only in 9 and 12; B only in 11 and 14; wardrobe identical), anonymous riders and defenders. Rider fire angles diagonally forward-left into the train; there is NO separate establishing shot — the escort riders and the firefight inside each frame carry the geography. The three sketches control composition and direction only, written corrections take priority; the render is photoreal, never sketch-like. Revolver: fired once in 1, half-lowered in 3, holstered in 5. Backward shots (1, 3, 5, 8) contain no bridge, arch or tunnel. In 6 he starts SEATED and rises on camera. No carriages ever behind the last car; its rear platform empty. No gunfire on the roofs: A's revolver never clears leather; both guards stay down on the roof. leeves%2C+flat+cap+%E2%80%94+hauls+up+onto+the+roof%2C+hand+going+for+his+revolver%3B+before+it+clears+leather+she+reaches+him%2C+pins+the+draw+arm%2C+and+a+fast+compact+hand-to-hand+fight+erupts+on+the+swaying+roof.+Cut+mid-exchange.%0ASHOT+10+%E2%80%94+18.6-19.8s+%E2%80%94+HIS+REACTION%2C+backward+axis%2C+47%C2%B0.+Frontal+tracking+close-up%3A+he+runs+toward+camera+along+the+FOURTH+roof%2C+the+roofs+behind+him+completely+EMPTY+%E2%80%94+only+bare+receding+roofline+and+sea+both+sides.+Mid-stride+his+eyes+lock+past+camera+on+her+fight+%E2%80%94+rhythm+stumbles+half+a+step%2C+breath+catches%3A+fear%2C+for+the+first+time%2C+aimed+at+someone+else%3B+he+pushes+harder.+Cut+on+the+acceleration.%0ASHOT+11+%E2%80%94+19.8-21.4s+%E2%80%94+HIS+OWN+AMBUSH%2C+forward+axis%2C+63%C2%B0.+Camera+behind+him+at+roof+level%3A+his+running+back+centered+on+the+fourth+roof%3B+at+the+gap+GUARD+B+%E2%80%94+brown+coat%2C+bowler+%E2%80%94+climbs+up+and+seizes+his+lapels%3A+a+graceless+desperate+grapple%2C+the+hero+bent+back+over+the+roof+edge%2C+boots+sliding%2C+clawing+for+hold%3B+mid-struggle+he+steals+one+wild+glance+toward+her+%E2%80%94+and+nearly+pays+for+it.+Cut+inside+the+struggle.%0ASHOT+12+%E2%80%94+21.4-23.2s+%E2%80%94+SHE+FINISHES+IT%2C+WALKS+TO+THE+EDGE%2C+SLIGHTLY+LATERAL+angle%2C+63%C2%B0.+Camera+at+roof+level+beside-and-behind+her+fight+%E2%80%94+a+light+side+offset%2C+not+a+straight+rear+view%3A+GUARD+A+lunges+with+everything%3B+she+sidesteps%2C+hooks+the+arm%2C+uses+his+own+momentum+%E2%80%94+one+clean+economical+throw%2C+and+he+crumples+flat%2C+out+cold%2C+dead+weight+rocking+with+the+sway.+She+doesn%27t+even+straighten+her+hat.+Unhurried%2C+she+walks+diagonally+to+the+roof%27s+RIGHT+edge+and+stops+at+the+brink%2C+wind+pulling+her+duster%2C+the+sea+far+below.+Cut+as+she+stops.%0ASHOT+13+%E2%80%94+23.2-25.2s+%E2%80%94+HER+FAREWELL+SMILE%2C+close-up%2C+slow+push-in%2C+47%C2%B0.+From+behind+and+outboard+of+her+right+shoulder+at+the+third+roof%27s+right+edge%3A+hat%2C+streaming+hair+and+shoulder+dominate%2C+sea+and+arch+defocused+in+oval+bokeh%3B+as+the+camera+creeps+in+she+turns+her+head+back+over+her+RIGHT+shoulder+%E2%80%94+almost+into+the+lens%2C+sun+catching+her+eyes+%E2%80%94+finds+him+one+carriage+back.+One+LIGHT%2C+thin%2C+FLIRTY+farewell+smile+past+camera+%E2%80%94+playful%2C+teasing%2C+barely-there+%E2%80%94+held+a+beat+at+the+tightest+point%3B+her+only+look+back.+Then+she+turns+away+to+the+sea.+Cut+on+the+turn.%0ASHOT+14+%E2%80%94+25.2-27.0s+%E2%80%94+HIS+KO%2C+HER+JUMP+IN+THE+BACKGROUND%2C+forward+axis%2C+63%C2%B0.+Behind+his+grapple%3A+he+and+GUARD+B+torque+at+center%3B+in+the+bright+depth+one+roof+ahead%2C+small+at+frame-right%2C+she+stands+at+the+right+edge.+Half-strangled%2C+he+winds+up+one+wild+graceless+haymaker+%E2%80%94+it+CONNECTS%3B+B+drops+like+a+sack%2C+out+cold.+In+those+same+seconds+her+small+figure+steps+off+the+edge+and+drops+below+the+rooflines.+He+straightens+panting+%E2%80%94+head+snapping+toward+where+she+vanished.+Cut+on+the+snap.%0ASHOT+15+%E2%80%94+27.0-28.6s+%E2%80%94+AERIAL+WIDE%3A+THE+DIVE+AND+THE+TUNNEL%2C+84%C2%B0.+High+off+the+train%27s+right+over+the+sea%2C+tracking%3A+the+full+dark-red+train+with+bright+steam+crosses+the+viaduct+left-to-right%3B+at+frame-right+the+locomotive+calmly+enters+the+ordinary+tunnel+portal+in+the+headland%3B+she+commits+into+a+clean+confident+dive+%E2%80%94+one+hand+pressing+her+hat%2C+duster+and+hair+streaming+%E2%80%94+a+long+accelerating+fall+past+the+grand+arch%3B+a+small+hard+white+splash+blooms%3B+on+the+third+roof%27s+right+edge+his+tiny+figure+stays+planted%2C+head+tracking+her+all+the+way+down+as+carriages+feed+into+the+tunnel.+Cut+on+the+splash.%0ASHOT+16+%E2%80%94+28.6-30.0s+%E2%80%94+HIS+LOSS+AND+THE+DARK%2C+close-up+FROM+BEHIND%2C+forward+axis%2C+47%C2%B0.+Roof-level+tracking+close-up+framed+FROM+BEHIND+HIM%3A+his+BACK+to+camera+large+in+the+foreground%2C+body+angled+a+touch+off-axis+%E2%80%94+and+his+head+turned+BACK+over+his+RIGHT+shoulder+toward+the+camera%2C+so+his+FACE+reads+clearly+in+3%2F4+past+that+right+shoulder%3B+chin+dropped%2C+gaze+aimed+steeply+DOWN+past+the+tail+%E2%80%94+well+below+the+horizon+%E2%80%94+locked+on+the+fading+white+splash+ring%3B+chest+heaving%3B+ahead+of+him%2C+deeper+in+the+frame%2C+the+dark+tunnel+mouth+closes+in.+Mild+surprise+sinks+into+the+feeling+of+a+MISSED+CHANCE+%E2%80%94+hope+visibly+draining%2C+shoulders+easing+down%2C+self-directed+disappointment+plain+on+his+face%3B+he+doesn%27t+move%2C+doesn%27t+duck%2C+nothing+touches+him+%E2%80%94+the+train+simply+carries+him+into+the+tunnel%3A+darkness+slides+over+and+swallows+the+frame.+Hard+cut+to+black+inside+the+tunnel+roar.+End.%0AACTING%0AScene+event+%28unspoken%29%3A+a+dare+that+cannot+be+refused%2C+closed+by+a+goodbye+nobody+names.+HERO+%E2%80%94+a+trying+man%2C+not+a+cool+one%3A+diligent%2C+a+little+untidy%2C+small+honest+doubts+and+a+light+fear+he+pushes+through%2C+never+comedic%3B+the+overtake+is+the+world+saying+no+and+his+face+quietly+refusing+it%3B+the+untidiness+lives+in+his+manner%2C+never+in+the+leap%3B+the+climb+is+stubbornness+beating+gravity%3B+in+shot+10+fear+points+outward+for+the+first+time+and+makes+him+faster%3B+the+haymaker+astonishes+him%3B+16+is+not+grief+%E2%80%94+the+smaller+ache+of+a+missed+chance%2C+darkness+arriving+while+he+still+looks.+HEROINE+%E2%80%94+she+performs+the+robbery+for+an+audience+of+one%3A+the+shot%2C+the+unmasking%2C+the+smirk+held+a+beat+too+long+are+aimed+at+him%3B+after+it+she+never+checks+%E2%80%94+not+checking+is+the+flex%3B+the+roof+fight+is+housework%3B+the+one+look+back+is+the+only+goodbye+she+will+spend%2C+turned+away+before+an+answer+can+form%3B+the+dive+is+the+signature%3A+no+hesitation%2C+hat+pressed+like+tipping+it.+She+never+speaks.+Guards+play+%22hold+the+roof%22%2C+dead+weight+when+out%3B+riders+play+%22win+the+firefight%22%2C+never+watching+the+leads.+%28Gazes+task-engaged%2C+natural+blink+cadence%2C+no+frozen+stares.%29%0APHYSICS+%2F+AUDIO+%2F+STYLE%0AFull-speed+train+and+four-beat+gallop%3B+his+drift+relative+to+the+train+smooth+and+readable%2C+never+a+jump%3B+the+rise+to+standing+genuinely+unstable%3B+both+leaps+carry+real+launch%2C+arc+and+cloth+drag%3B+the+miss+is+physically+true+%E2%80%94+he+lands+short+on+the+rear+platform+below+frame%2C+then+a+muscle-real+pull-up+to+chest+height%3B+roof+fights+fight+the+sway%3B+the+stopped+draw+is+a+real+grip+contest%3B+the+throw+uses+the+attacker%27s+momentum%3B+her+dive%3A+real+push-off%2C+clean+arc%2C+long+fall%2C+one+small+hard+splash.+No+blood.+Audio%3A+pack+hooves%2C+gunfire+with+cliff+echo+%28ground+only%29%2C+wind%3B+muffled+slow-motion+wash%2C+roaring+back+on+the+cut%3B+a+muffled+impact+below+frame+at+the+miss%2C+then+only+wind+and+wheels+through+the+pause%3B+hollow+viaduct+drumming%3B+fight+thuds+and+wordless+effort+%E2%80%94+no+dialogue+or+gunfire+on+the+roofs%3B+the+whistling+drop%2C+one+distant+splash%3B+the+tunnel+roar+swallowing+all+to+black.+His+voice+only+the+yelp+in+shot+2%3B+no+music%2C+no+narration%2C+no+subtitles.+Style%3A+widescreen+western+action+realism%2C+35mm+film%2C+Kodak+500T+%E2%80%94+fine+grain%2C+slightly+muted+tones%2C+gently+pulled-down+exposure+keeping+the+hard+sunny+contrast%2C+1880s+detail%3B+sharp+faces%2C+natural+180%C2%B0+shutter+blur%2C+no+ghosting%2C+duplicated+limbs+or+flicker.%0ACONSTRAINTS%0AExactly+two+named+characters%2C+two+guards+%28A+only+in+9+and+12%3B+B+only+in+11+and+14%3B+wardrobe+identical%29%2C+anonymous+riders+and+defenders.+Rider+fire+angles+diagonally+forward-left+into+the+train%3B+there+is+NO+separate+establishing+shot+%E2%80%94+the+escort+riders+and+the+firefight+inside+each+frame+carry+the+geography.+The+three+sketches+control+composition+and+direction+only%2C+written+corrections+take+priority%3B+the+render+is+photoreal%2C+never+sketch-like.+Revolver%3A+fired+once+in+1%2C+half-lowered+in+3%2C+holstered+in+5.+Backward+shots+%281%2C+3%2C+5%2C+8%29+contain+no+bridge%2C+arch+or+tunnel.+In+6+he+starts+SEATED+and+rises+on+camera.+No+carriages+ever+behind+the+last+car%3B+its+rear+platform+empty.+No+gunfire+on+the+roofs%3A+A%27s+revolver+never+clears+leather%3B+both+guards+stay+down+on+the+roof.+She+looks+back+only+in+13+and+never+speaks%3B+her+dive+starts+in+14%27s+background%2C+is+covered+fully+in+15%2C+and+she+never+reappears.+The+tunnel+is+ordinary%2C+never+dangerous%3B+he+doesn%27t+duck+and+is+never+struck.+Hats+stay+on.+Slow+motion+only+in+shot+7.+The+train+never+stops+or+slows.+No+insects.+The+piece+ends+on+black+inside+the+tunnel.)

### Scene 5 — The Search

**What it does:** Animates the opening of the masked ball, ending on her one deliberate look in slow motion.

**Assets:** @Nate\_carnavil, @Aila\_carnival, @carnival

Style: 8K IMAX. Photorealistic — no 3D render, no game engine. Shot on 35mm anamorphic film: horizontal squeeze and compression, oval / elliptical bokeh with stretched highlights on the lantern strings and torch flames, curved barrel edge distortion, chromatic aberration toward the edges — all subtle-to-moderate, gentle. NO lens flares, NO streaks, NO floating bokeh circles, no out-of-focus objects parked in front of the lens. Focus falloff only where the shot calls for it — the GIRL always reads sharp.

Lighting: NIGHT, a colossal street carnival in old Italy at full blaze. Criss-crossing strings of multicolored paper lanterns and warm bulb garlands hang high over the whole piazza; the palazzo facade glows from every tall window; torches and iron wall-lanterns burn along the arcades; golden ground spark fountains hiss inside the crowd; FIREWORKS burst over the rooflines and the campanile — gold and warm-white blooms against the black sky. No fireworks or sparks ever appear inside the palazzo or behind its doors. The whole square is washed in warm festival light — every face readable, no crushed shadows. A soft night breeze sways the lantern strings and banners.

Color: 60:30:10 — warm festival gold on stone and crowd / black night sky / multicolor lantern glow and jewel-tone costumes. Burgundy-red gowns belong EXCLUSIVELY to the GIRL — no other woman in red.

Camera: 15-SECOND GENERATION, FIVE DYNAMIC SHOTS — fast-cut rhythm, every cut lands on action, no dead frames. 180° shutter motion blur, 24fps smooth motion, stable controlled moves, no jitter. One controlled slow-motion passage in SHOT 3. Every camera move is motivated by an action in the frame.

Skin: Pore-level realism — vellus hair, asymmetric moles, capillary flush, pore-shadow matching practical light.

Acting: Performance is governed by the ACTING TASK blocks below — nobody plays an emotion, everyone plays their task; the feelings arrive through the tasks. Nobody on the piazza ever stands frozen — the whole square lives in every frame.

Physics: Gravity and inertia respected — real weight, correct contact shadows, capes and gowns move with momentum, breeze acts on lanterns, banners, flames and sparks.

Continuity: Characters, props, environment identical across every cut. Every crowd member has a unique individual face, mask, costume and body type — no duplicated figures anywhere. There is NO MUSIC anywhere on the timeline.

Audio: NO music of any kind — no score, no orchestra, no melody, no humming. The carnival roars instead: a sea of voices, laughter and cheers, dance steps and stomping on stone, applause greeting the fireworks, the hiss of spark fountains, booms and crackle overhead, rustle of capes and silk, jester bells, the HERO's breath. No subtitles.

Characters (both adults in their mid-20s):

HERO — man with short neat dark hair, dark brown eyes, clean-shaven. Deep-burgundy baroque frock coat with black satin peak lapels and long damask-patterned scalloped tails, burgundy damask waistcoat, striped shirt, burgundy silk cravat with silver brooch, black leather gloves, black pinstriped trousers. His black-and-gold venetian eye mask is worn over his eyes AT ALL TIMES — never removed, never pushed up; his eyes stay fully visible and readable through the mask openings. He has NEVER met the GIRL — tonight he only hopes to. He NEVER RUNS anywhere in this scene — he walks in a hurry: quick, light, eager strides, never a running gait, never aggression; he weaves, sidesteps, apologizes with his hands, never shoves anyone.

GIRL — woman with dark hair in a soft romantic updo with loose face-framing strands and small gold pins, deep brown eyes. Opulent red-burgundy ball gown with gold-beaded off-the-shoulder bodice, ornate red-and-gold choker collar, sheer champagne bell sleeves, cream embroidered central skirt panel. A GOLD FILIGREE venetian eye mask with small red gem accents over her eyes, tied with a gold ribbon, her eyes fully visible through it. The ONLY woman in red at the whole carnival. She is NOT dancing with anyone — she moves THROUGH the crowd, walking unhurried, letting the throng carry and conceal her.

GIRLS COMPANY — an episodic group of four-five young masked women in jewel-tone carnival gowns (emerald, sapphire, violet, gold — no red), laughing, playful; they materialize from the crowd, never waiting in frame.

CROWD — a SEA of people filling the entire piazza: a packed festive costumed throng in classic old-Italian carnival attire — bauta and colombina masks, gilded full-face masks, tricorn hats, hooded capes, jesters with bells, plague-doctor masks, powdered wigs, feathered headdresses, embroidered doublets and gowns. People dance in rings on the open stone, stroll in masked pairs, cluster laughing by the arcades and the fountain, crowd the balconies of the arcaded gallery, raise glasses on the palazzo steps, applaud the fireworks; torch-bearers thread through. Costumes in warm and jewel festival tones: gold, ochre, emerald, sapphire, violet, teal, cream, silver — no red or burgundy GOWNS on any woman, no frock coats resembling the HERO's. Every face and mask unique — nobody resembles the HERO or the GIRL.

ACTING TASKS

SCENE DIRECTION (shared, unspoken): the carnival itself plays with him — the crowd is the labyrinth, and she is the thread.

ACTING TASK — HERO (he plays the task; the hope arrives on its own):

MOTIVE: he has never been introduced to her — he only saw her once, and tonight the carnival has thrown them into one square; lose her in this sea and he loses her for good.

GOAL: catch the glimpse of her and reach it before the crowd swallows it.

OBSTACLE: a thousand celebrating bodies between them — and the carnival's hands that keep catching him into its games; inside, the first words he would say to her still aren't ready.

TACTIC, moment to moment:

— out of the doors: the roar hits him — half a beat to take it in;

— from behind, facing the sea of the carnival: his head turns in sectors, left, right, reading the square like a map, looking for one figure in ten thousand;

— the glimpse: something red between the bodies — his breath catches first, feet commit second;

— the hurried walk: quick light strides, never a run — the hurried walk of a man late for his own luck — reading the crowd two bodies ahead, weaving through gaps, palms raised in quick apologies;

— enclosed by the GIRLS COMPANY: he stops only because the ring closes — and being locked in the middle of strangers' fun makes him AWKWARD: an embarrassed half-smile, a small apologetic shrug, hands half-raised, eyes darting for a gap in the turning ring while his head keeps stealing glances toward where the red gown vanished;

— the escape: he picks his moment and carefully DUCKS THROUGH under the girls' linked arms — polite, nimble, almost apologetic — and is out, walking fast out of the frame, eyes back on the search.

(Safety: gaze always engaged in the task, warm and hopeful through the mask openings — never a frozen glassy stare, never aggression; natural blink cadence.)

ACTING TASK — GIRL (the close-up is hers; the smile must be earned, not worn):

MOTIVE: the carnival is her element — being found here must be earned.

GOAL: let him see her exactly once — and make the crowd hide her again.

OBSTACLE: to turn to him too soon is to give the game away; to not turn at all is to lose him.

TACTIC, moment to moment:

— she crosses the crowd unhurried, LEFT TO RIGHT across the frame, in PROFILE to the camera, eyes ahead — she already senses she is being watched, and makes him wait;

— then the SMALL TURN of her head toward the camera — unhurried, deliberate, a held breath of drama;

— her eyes find the lens — find HIM — and hold, calm and knowing, while passing revelers slide between them like curtains;

— and only then she allows it: a light, sweet smile at the CORNER of her lips — small, private, the smile of someone letting him know he was seen;

— a passerby wipes the frame — she lets the crowd close over her, never speeding up.

(Safety: eyes alive and engaged on the lens, natural blinks, never frozen, the smile stays small — never a full grin to camera.)

ACTING TASK — GIRLS COMPANY: MOTIVE: a handsome masked stranger hurrying through a carnival is fair game. GOAL: make him the center of their dance. OBSTACLE: he is already promised to someone across the square. TACTIC: they flock to him from the crowd, swirl around him and VISIBLY close the ring — hands linking one by one around him — then circle him like a khorovod, skirts flaring, laughing, spinning the ring around the awkward stranger at its center; when he ducks under their arms and slips out, they let him go with theatrical mock-offense and burst out laughing after him. (Safety: eyes engaged, natural blinks, the ring keeps real dance rhythm.)

ACTING TASK — CROWD: they live their own carnival at full roar — dancing rings, toasts, laughter, applause at every burst — nobody ever notices the HERO's pursuit. (Safety: constant motion, alive group behavior.)

### Scene 5 — The Parade

**What it does:** Animates the crossroad beat: the two leads separated by an endless parade, and the carriage pass that makes him vanish.

**Assets:** @Nate\_carnival, @Aila\_carnival, @carnival

Style: 8K IMAX. Photorealistic — no 3D render, no game engine. 35mm anamorphic film: horizontal squeeze, oval bokeh with stretched highlights on lantern strings and torch flames, subtle barrel distortion and edge chromatic aberration. NO lens flares, NO streaks, NO floating bokeh circles; nothing parked out-of-focus before the lens except the wipes written into the shots.

Lighting: NIGHT, a colossal street carnival in old Italy at full blaze. Strings of multicolored paper lanterns criss-cross high over the piazza; the palazzo windows glow; torches and wall-lanterns burn along the arcades; golden spark fountains hiss in the crowd; fireworks bloom over the rooflines and campanile. Warm festival light everywhere — every face readable, no crushed shadows. A soft breeze moves lanterns, banners and flames.

RULES (Continuity, in force in every shot):

1\. SEPARATION — HIGHEST PRIORITY: the parade's road is EIGHT METERS WIDE; each lead stands about ONE METER back from its edge, pressed into the front row of their bank — the HERO and the GIRL stand TEN METERS APART, directly opposite each other, the procession always between them. The PARADE is already marching at frame one and NEVER stops, pauses, thins or ends — a continuous river at a steady pace through every shot including the last frame; eight meters filled shoulder to shoulder, rank behind rank, no usable gaps; endlessly long, stretching beyond the frame both ways. 3\. NOBODY ever walks or passes behind the leads' backs — the parade exists only in the road between the banks. Behind each lead: only standing spectators.

4\. BACKGROUND DEPTH: behind each lead the square CONTINUES — rows of standing spectators recede in soft layers, and beyond them the piazza breathes in the blur: lantern light, distant arcades, more crowd. Never a wall, facade or blank surface behind either lead; the crowd is backed against nothing.

5\. CROWD ATTENTION: the parade is the main show — every spectator watches THE PARADE, absorbed; nobody ever looks at the HERO or the GIRL.

6\. BANKS: both leads stand FLUSH in their front rows, spectators pressed at their left, right and back; neither ever stands ahead of the row or on the road; the two banks mirror each other in density and depth.

His black-and-gold venetian eye mask is on his eyes AT ALL TIMES — never removed, never pushed up, his eyes fully readable through it. PARADE — the carnival's main spectacle: drummers with big carnival drums; torch-bearers; banner-carriers with tall poles and streaming pennants; costumed marchers; CIRCUS STILT-WALKERS towering over the flow; ornate CHARIOTS — festival floats on great wooden wheels drawn by costumed teams, carrying performers and lanterns; tall ORNATE CARRIAGES drawn by pairs of adorned horses, their bodies rising above the marchers; GIANT gold-and-ochre PUPPETS swaying on poles. Warm and jewel tones, no red or burgundy garments.

ACTING TASKS

SCENE DIRECTION (shared, unspoken): a crossroad — he on one side, she directly opposite, ten meters and an endless procession between them; the whole square watches the road, only these two watch each other; the vanishing is his move in her game.

HERO — MOTIVE: she is right there, straight across the road, and their eyes have finally met. GOAL: get across to her. OBSTACLE: eight meters of marching wall with no gaps; hope keeps offering seams, the river keeps closing them. TACTIC: through the crush in a straight line — sideways between shoulders, a hand raised in apology; wedge into the front row, find her through the flashing slivers, hold the eye contact; try the wall — a half-step at a seam that closes, a lean at the next gap a rank of drummers erases, rising on his toes to keep her in sight, try after try; and when the carriage crosses his sightline — simply GONE: not fleeing, not stepping back — vanished, the carnival's own trick, played by him now. (Safety: gaze warm and hopeful through the mask, never a frozen glassy stare; every move polite and nimble, never a shove.)

GIRL — MOTIVE: the game was hers all evening — and there he is, found. GOAL: let him know she is GLAD; then, when he vanishes, find him again. OBSTACLE: the never-ending procession; then his absence where he just stood. TACTIC: hold his eyes across the flow between the passing bodies, calm — and allow a sweet smile at the CORNER of her lips, small, warm, unmistakable; when the slivers show only strangers — search: left, right, rising on her toes, quick small head turns, light confusion first; the search comes back empty — the confusion settles into faint sadness, her head drops slightly, one quiet beat; then TURN and slip INTO the crowd behind her — the crowd does NOT part, no corridor opens: she squeezes between the packed bodies herself until the throng closes around the red gown. (Safety: eyes alive, natural blinks; the smile small and true; the sadness a settling, never a performance to camera.)

PARADE — the carnival itself: drummers locked in the beat, stilt-walkers striding, chariot teams hauling, carriage horses stepping steady, puppets swaying in rhythm; they notice no one, stop for no one, never run out. (Safety: constant marching pace, real weight, continuous flow in every frame.)

CROWD — every face on the procession, cheering, gasping at the stilt-walkers and carriages; nobody looks at the leads, nobody opens a path. (Safety: constant small motion, alive group behavior.)

sing+ONLY+%E2%80%94+it+is+NOT+a+keyframe+and+sets+no+camera+position%3B+every+shot+builds+its+own+viewpoint.+TOTAL+RUNTIME+15+SECONDS%2C+FOUR+SHOTS.%0ASHOT+1+%E2%80%94+AXIS%3A+camera+behind+the+HERO%2C+straight+into+the+crowd+to+the+front+row%2C+63%C2%B0+FOV+%280%E2%80%933s%29%3A%0AThe+camera+follows+BEHIND+the+HERO+%28match+%3C%3C%3C932441d0-4ea7-4982-a982-05486edcaa6a%3E%3E%3E%29+on+the+hero-girl+axis+as+he+moves+in+a+PERFECTLY+STRAIGHT+LINE+away+from+the+lens+INTO+the+concert-dense+crush+%E2%80%94+sideways%2C+edging+between+shoulders%2C+people+shifting+just+enough%2C+the+camera+pressing+after+him+through+the+same+gap+%E2%80%94+no+arc%2C+no+detour.+He+WEDGES+into+the+front+row+and+STOPS+a+meter+from+the+flow+%E2%80%94+spectators+at+his+left%2C+right+and+back+%E2%80%94+and+before+him+the+PARADE+crosses+the+frame+horizontally%2C+left+to+right%3A+drummers%2C+a+stilt-walker+striding+high%2C+a+glowing+chariot%2C+a+giant+puppet+swaying+%E2%80%94+and+through+the+flashing+slivers%2C+DIRECTLY+OPPOSITE%2C+ten+meters+away%3A+the+GIRL+%28match+%3C%3C%3C8d7e5287-98b7-444b-919f-ab85c7340877%3E%3E%3E%29%2C+pressed+into+her+own+front+row%2C+the+red+gown%2C+the+square%27s+glow+deep+behind+her+%E2%80%94+and+their+eyes+find+each+other+across+the+road.+Roar+of+the+crush%2C+steady+drums%2C+wheels+on+stone%2C+his+caught+breath+%E2%80%94+no+music.%0ASHOT+2+%E2%80%94+FRONTAL+across+the+road%3A+the+GIRL%2C+eyes+meet%2C+a+sweet+smile%2C+47%C2%B0+FOV+%283%E2%80%935s%29%3A%0AHead-on+from+his+bank%3A+the+GIRL+%28match+%3C%3C%3C8d7e5287-98b7-444b-919f-ab85c7340877%3E%3E%3E%29+at+ten+meters%2C+seen+THROUGH+the+passing+marchers+sweeping+the+near+foreground+as+dark+wipes%2C+thin+flashing+slivers+between+them+%E2%80%94+pressed+into+her+front+row%2C+spectators+at+her+shoulders%2C+the+crowd+receding+in+soft+layers+into+the+piazza%27s+blurred+glow+behind+her.+In+the+slivers+her+eyes+MEET+his%3A+she+holds+the+look%2C+calm+and+steady+%E2%80%94+and+a+sweet+smile+arrives+at+the+CORNER+of+her+lips%2C+small+and+warm+%E2%80%94+she+is+GLAD+to+see+him.+The+gold+filigree+mask+glints+with+each+reveal.+Drums%2C+boots+on+stone%2C+cheers+%E2%80%94+no+music.%0ASHOT+3+%E2%80%94+FRONTAL+across+the+road%3A+the+HERO%27s+attempts%2C+the+CARRIAGE+PASS+%E2%80%94+GONE%2C+long+hold+on+the+empty+place%2C+47%C2%B0+FOV+%285%E2%80%9311.5s%29%3A%0AThe+reverse%2C+head-on+from+her+bank%3A+the+HERO+%28match+%3C%3C%3C932441d0-4ea7-4982-a982-05486edcaa6a%3E%3E%3E%29+at+ten+meters+beyond+the+flow%2C+wedged+frontally+in+his+front+row%2C+the+crowd+and+the+piazza%27s+blurred+depth+behind+him+%E2%80%94+never+a+wall.+FULL+OF+HOPE+he+works+the+wall%3A+a+half-step+at+a+seam+that+closes+instantly%2C+a+lean+at+the+next+gap+a+rank+of+drummers+erases%2C+rising+on+his+toes+to+hold+her+in+sight+%E2%80%94+try+after+try%2C+unhurried+beats%2C+the+river+giving+nothing.+Then+an+ORNATE+CARRIAGE+rolling+with+the+procession+ENTERS+FRAME+LEFT+%E2%80%94+tall%2C+drawn+by+a+pair+of+adorned+horses%2C+its+high+body+rising+above+the+marchers+%E2%80%94+it+crosses+and+COMPLETELY+COVERS+the+HERO+for+a+long+beat...+and+when+its+rear+CLEARS+FRAME+RIGHT%3A+the+HERO+IS+NO+LONGER+THERE.+The+camera+HOLDS+on+the+empty+place+%E2%80%94+beat+after+beat%3A+strangers%27+masked+faces+watching+the+parade%2C+the+packed+row+closed+where+he+stood%2C+the+square+still+breathing+behind+%E2%80%94+he+did+not+run%2C+he+did+not+step+away%3A+he+simply+VANISHED.+The+parade+keeps+flowing.+Drums%2C+hooves+and+wheels+rolling+away%2C+the+roar+%E2%80%94+no+music.%0ASHOT+4+%E2%80%94+FRONTAL+on+the+GIRL%3A+search%2C+confusion%2C+sadness%2C+leaving+%E2%80%94+no+corridor%2C+47%C2%B0+FOV+%2811.5%E2%80%9315s%29%3A%0AHead-on+medium+on+the+GIRL+%28match+%3C%3C%3C8d7e5287-98b7-444b-919f-ab85c7340877%3E%3E%3E%29+in+her+front+row%2C+the+parade+still+crossing+before+her%3A+she+SEARCHES+with+her+eyes+%E2%80%94+left%2C+right%2C+along+the+opposite+bank+through+the+slivers%2C+rising+on+her+toes%2C+quick+small+head+turns+%E2%80%94+light+confusion%2C+hope+still+working%3B+the+search+comes+back+empty+%E2%80%94+faint+SADNESS+settles%2C+her+head+drops+slightly%2C+eyes+lowering%2C+one+quiet+beat.+She+TURNS+and+slips+INTO+the+dense+crowd+behind+her+%E2%80%94+no+corridor%2C+nobody+steps+aside%3A+she+squeezes+between+the+packed+bodies%2C+shoulders+turning%2C+and+the+throng+CLOSES+around+the+red+gown+until+she+is+GONE+from+frame.+The+drums+still+crossing+behind%2C+the+roar+swallowing+her+steps+%E2%80%94+no+music.)

* * *

### Scene 5 — The Touch

**What it does:** Animates the payoff: her search, the stop, and the slow-motion fingertip touch that bridges into the elevator ding.

**Assets:** @Nate\_carnival, @Aila\_carnival, @carnival

Style: 8K IMAX. Photorealistic — no 3D render, no game engine. 35mm anamorphic film: horizontal squeeze, oval bokeh with stretched highlights on lantern strings, subtle barrel distortion and edge chromatic aberration. NO lens flares, NO streaks, NO floating bokeh circles. The only out-of-focus foreground elements in the whole film are the ones written into the shots: the standing spectators' bodies passing across the lens in SHOT 1, and the HERO's blurred silhouette in the depth in SHOT 2.

Lighting: NIGHT, the old-Italian street carnival at full blaze — deep inside the standing crowd: warm lantern glow from the strings criss-crossing above, torch light flickering between bodies, the glow of the performance in the middle of the square deep in the frame. Warm festival light, her face always readable, no crushed shadows.

Color: 60:30:10 — warm festival gold / black night sky / multicolor lanterns and jewel-tone costumes. Red-burgundy belongs EXCLUSIVELY to the GIRL — no other woman in red anywhere.

Camera: 15-SECOND GENERATION, THREE SHOTS — every cut lands on action, no dead frames. 180° shutter, 24fps, stable moves, no jitter — with ONE written exception: SHOT 3 plays in GENTLE SLOW MOTION. COMPOSITION: every frame is composed by the RULE OF THIRDS and the GOLDEN RATIO — key subjects sit on the third lines and golden-section intersections, never dead-center by accident; in close-ups her eyes ride the upper third line with balanced headroom; walking and looking directions get lead room; the frame breathes, deliberately weighted. Every camera move is motivated by an action in the frame.

Skin: pore-level realism, natural texture, capillary flush, pore-shadow matching practical light.

Acting: governed by the ACTING TASK blocks — nobody plays an emotion, everyone plays their task. SHOT 2 is one continuous orbiting shot, no cut; its three events — the orbit ending frontal, her stop, the silhouette appearing — happen AT THE SAME MOMENT.

Physics: real weight and inertia — gowns with momentum, real contact shadows, breeze on lanterns.

Continuity: direct continuation of the previous scene — the GIRL has left the parade and moves deeper into the square's audience, hunting for the man who vanished.

STANDING AUDIENCE RULE: the crowd does NOT move — every extra STANDS STILL where they are, backs to the camera, faces turned away toward the performance in the middle of the square; packed dense, shoulder to shoulder, no bare stone, no gaps, no thinning. Nobody walks, strolls, crosses the frame, enters or exits frame, moves between rows, steps aside, follows her or walks along with her; no corridor ever opens. They are alive only IN PLACE — small weight shifts, heads tilting to see past the head in front, shoulders settling, hands clapping and raised toward the show, hats and feathers bobbing — but their feet never travel. The GIRL is the ONLY person in motion in every shot and the only face turned toward the camera.

CROWD ATTENTION RULE — ABSOLUTE: the spectators are completely absorbed in the procession and the performance at the centre of the square, and NOTHING else exists for them. Not one person notices the GIRL at any moment in any shot: nobody looks at her, nobody glances her way, nobody turns their head toward her, nobody follows her with their eyes, nobody reacts to her passing, nobody makes room for her, nobody speaks to her, nobody looks into the camera. Even when she slips right between two of them, both keep facing the show without registering her. She passes through this crowd unseen and unremarked, invisible to every single one of them — the whole square is watching something else.

She moves between the standing bodies WITHOUT jostling — no shoulder-pushing, no hands laid on anyone: she slips gracefully through the narrow gaps; the frame stays CRAMPED, bodies edge to edge, but her passage is soft.

HANDS: BOTH LEADS' HANDS ARE BARE IN ALL THREE SHOTS — no gloves at any point. HER HAND RULE — HIGHEST PRIORITY IN SHOT 3: the GIRL's right arm hangs DOWN ALONG HER BODY, resting naturally against her side — the way a hand hangs in ordinary life: relaxed, loose, fingers softly curled. It is NOT extended, NOT offered, NOT held out, NOT lifted away from her body, NOT hovering, NOT posed to be taken — simply a hand at rest by her hip, and it stays exactly so until his touch lands. The ONLY moving hand in the frame is the HERO's — HE makes the first tactile contact of their story, entirely his move; her fingers respond ONLY AFTER his touch.

IDENTITY LOCK AT EVERY SCALE: the GIRL is the woman from @8d7e5287-98b7-444b-919f-ab85c7340877 and the HERO is the man from @932441d0-4ea7-4982-a982-05486edcaa6a in every shot and at every distance — close-up, medium or detail: same face, hair, costume, mask, never a stand-in, never another woman in a red gown. The HERO's black-and-gold venetian eye mask is on his eyes AT ALL TIMES; the GIRL's gold filigree mask with red gems on hers, eyes readable through both. No red or burgundy on anyone else; every extra unique. NO MUSIC anywhere on the timeline.

Audio: no score, no melody, no humming — only the carnival: the murmur of the standing crowd, cheers and applause rolling in from the middle of the square, the parade's drums DISTANT in the depth (percussion only, not music), silk brushing past sleeves, her breath close. Over the final frame the soundscape executes the written sound bridge. No subtitles.

Characters (both mid-20s):

GIRL — dark hair in a soft romantic updo with loose strands and small gold pins, deep brown eyes. Opulent red-burgundy ball gown: gold-beaded off-the-shoulder bodice, red-and-gold choker, sheer champagne bell sleeves, cream embroidered skirt panel, velvet train. Gold filigree venetian eye mask with small red gems, gold ribbon, her eyes fully visible. The only woman in red at the whole carnival. Her hands are BARE in all shots.

HERO — short neat dark hair, dark brown eyes, clean-shaven. Deep-burgundy baroque frock coat with black satin peak lapels, burgundy damask waistcoat, striped shirt, burgundy cravat with silver brooch, black pinstriped trousers. Black-and-gold venetian eye mask over his eyes, never removed. He NEVER RUNS; he arrives quiet and certain. He is COMPLETELY ABSENT from SHOT 1 — not in frame, not in the background, not glimpsed in any blur: the audience must not find him before she stops looking. In SHOT 2 he exists only as a BLURRED SILHOUETTE deep behind her. In SHOT 3 only his bare left hand is in frame. His hands are BARE.

CROWD — the festive audience of the carnival's main night: standing still, packed dense, backs to the camera, every face turned away toward the show in the centre of the square; old-Italian carnival attire in warm and jewel tones. Nobody notices the two leads.

ACTING TASKS

SCENE DIRECTION (shared, unspoken): the whole city stands facing the show — she alone moves through their backs, searching for the one man who is not there; and the heart understands before the eyes do: her stopping is her answer, and the first touch is his.

GIRL — one continuous line across the film: SEARCHING, the search running out, and the stop.

MOTIVE: he vanished the way SHE vanishes all evening — he is playing, so he is HERE, somewhere in this square, and she means to find him.

GOAL: pick his black-and-gold mask and burgundy coat out of a standing mass that shows her nothing but backs.

OBSTACLE: hundreds of people turned away from her, every one of them absorbed in something else; and the longer she looks, the more the square proves he is not where she can reach.

TACTIC — SHOT 1, the search at full strength: she works the crowd with her eyes, body by body, ruling each one out — checking the profile of the man she is passing and dropping him, looking along the row ahead, glancing down the next gap between shoulders, turning her head to take the row on her other side, rising onto her toes to scan over the heads. Her eyes are ALIVE and FULL OF HOPE — bright, quick, expectant, lifting at every dark coat and every gold-trimmed mask before she sees it is a stranger. She badly wants to find him, and each miss only sends her deeper.

TACTIC — SHOT 2, the search running out: she is still looking — still turning her head, still glancing between the standing bodies, still checking the faces she passes — but the looks come slower and hold shorter, each one answered by another stranger, and under the search a light upset and a slight sadness begin to surface: the face of someone who is starting to accept that she has lost him. Her steps hurry a little; the hope is going out of her eyes look by look.

TACTIC — the STOP: then, in ONE MOMENT — the moment she STOPS — it happens: she FEELS he is near, somewhere right behind her — and HER EYES FILL WITH HOPE, a short intake of breath, her chin lifting a touch — she does NOT turn: she stands still, letting him come, her stopping itself the invitation; both her arms simply hang down along her body as they always do, her right hand resting bare against her side — nothing in her posture asks or offers — and when his fingertip finds that resting hand, she does not startle: her heart arrived there first; a small breath, and only then her fingers answer softly.

HER HANDS in SHOTS 1 and 2 have their own work, and it keeps them off other people: one hand lifts and holds the front of her own skirt clear as she moves, the other stays close at her own side. She never reaches for, leans on or touches a single spectator.

(Safety: eyes alive with natural blinks throughout, never frozen or glassy; the searching is hopeful and intent, never frightened, never distressed, and she never calls out or reaches for anyone; the hope at the stop arrives as a breath and the eyes filling, small and true — never a gasp to camera.)

HERO — MOTIVE: she taught him the carnival's game — vanish behind the moving curtain, reappear where you are not expected. GOAL: arrive — not to catch her: to be there; the first touch of their story is HIS to make. TACTIC: he stays out of sight while she is hunting, and comes up quietly through the standing crowd behind her only once she has stopped looking — and his bare LEFT hand reaches DOWN toward her bare RIGHT hand resting at her side: his INDEX FINGER leading, extended ahead of the other fingers, the others relaxed and softly curled — and it is the PAD OF HIS INDEX FINGER that makes the touch, gently grazing her fingers: soft, warm, certain — skin to skin — an arrival; "I am here." (Safety: the touch gentle, never forceful; the reach unhurried.)

CROWD — one task and one only: WATCH THE SHOW IN THE MIDDLE OF THE SQUARE — standing still, absorbed, backs to the camera, cheering and applauding toward the centre. Nobody looks back, nobody notices the GIRL passing, nobody moves from their place. (Safety: alive stillness — small weight shifts, applause toward the square, never frozen statues, and never a step taken.)

Scene:

Deep inside the standing audience on the old-Italian carnival piazza from @796d6085-c6d8-4fdb-8525-85e9788ee9a8 at NIGHT — lantern strings above, the glow of the performance and the distant drums deep in the frame where every spectator faces. NOTE ON @796d6085-c6d8-4fdb-8525-85e9788ee9a8: architecture and festival dressing ONLY — not a keyframe, no camera position. TOTAL RUNTIME 15 SECONDS, THREE SHOTS. This film ends the scene: the final frame carries the sound bridge.

SHOT 1 — CLOSE-UP THROUGH THE CROWD: her face between the standing bodies as she searches, focus locked on her alone, 47° FOV (0–4.5s):

A CLOSE-UP of the GIRL (match @8d7e5287-98b7-444b-919f-ab85c7340877) seen THROUGH the crowd: the camera travels laterally with her at head height, deep inside the standing mass, and between the lens and her face pass the BODIES of the standing spectators — shoulders, backs, capes, hats and feathers sliding across the frame in the near FOREGROUND as heavy soft blurs, wiping over her and clearing again. They are not walking: they stand rooted, facing away toward the show, and it is the camera and the GIRL travelling past them that sweeps them across the frame.

FOCUS IS LOCKED ON HER ALONE for the whole shot: her masked face and her eyes stay critically sharp through every reveal, while everything in front of her and behind her — the foreground bodies, the depth of the crowd, the lantern strings, the far glow of the performance — falls into soft warm bokeh. She is composed on a third line with lead room in the direction she is moving.

She is SEARCHING and it is all in her eyes and her head: she checks the profile of the man she passes and drops him, looks along the row ahead, glances down the next gap between shoulders, turns her head to take the row on her other side, lifts her chin to see past the heads. Her eyes are ALIVE, BLINKING, BRIGHT AND FULL OF HOPE — quick and expectant, lifting at every dark coat before she sees it is a stranger, then moving on. She badly wants to find him.

NOT ONE spectator notices her: every body she passes stays turned toward the show, absorbed, cheering toward the centre of the square; nobody glances at her, nobody turns a head, nobody makes room. The HERO is NOWHERE in this shot — not in frame, not in the blur, nowhere. Murmur of the standing crowd, applause rolling in from the centre, distant drums — no music.

SHOT 2 — ONE CONTINUOUS ORBIT WITH PUSH-IN: medium profile → super close-up frontal; the STOP, the ORBIT'S END and the SILHOUETTE land in ONE MOMENT, 47° FOV (4.5–11s):

Cut to a wider scale than the previous shot: it opens MEDIUM, in PROFILE, her whole head and shoulders and the gown's bodice in frame, the standing crowd's backs around her now clearly readable rather than blurred wipes — the GIRL (match @8d7e5287-98b7-444b-919f-ab85c7340877) walking through the dense standing mass IN A SLIGHT HURRY, light quick steps past the motionless backs, she alone in motion, slipping through the gaps without jostling and without touching anyone.

SHE IS STILL SEARCHING as she walks: she keeps turning her head to check the faces she passes, glances between the standing bodies, looks along the rows to her left and to her right, still hunting him among the masks. But the search is RUNNING OUT — her looks come slower and hold shorter, each one answered by another stranger, and under them a light upset and a slight sadness surface look by look: the hope is going out of her eyes as the shot progresses. Her eyes stay BLINKING and ALIVE the whole time.

The camera ORBITS her as she walks AND PUSHES IN at the same time — gliding around from her profile toward her front while CLOSING from medium tighter and tighter, the crowd's backs sliding past the frame edges. %94+nothing+in+her+posture+asks+or+offers+%E2%80%94+and+when+his+fingertip+finds+that+resting+hand%2C+she+does+not+startle%3A+her+heart+arrived+there+first%3B+a+small+breath%2C+and+only+then+her+fingers+answer+softly.%0AHER+HANDS+in+SHOTS+1+and+2+have+their+own+work%2C+and+it+keeps+them+off+other+people%3A+one+hand+lifts+and+holds+the+front+of+her+own+skirt+clear+as+she+moves%2C+the+other+stays+close+at+her+own+side.+She+never+reaches+for%2C+leans+on+or+touches+a+single+spectator.%0A%28Safety%3A+eyes+alive+with+natural+blinks+throughout%2C+never+frozen+or+glassy%3B+the+searching+is+hopeful+and+intent%2C+never+frightened%2C+never+distressed%2C+and+she+never+calls+out+or+reaches+for+anyone%3B+the+hope+at+the+stop+arrives+as+a+breath+and+the+eyes+filling%2C+small+and+true+%E2%80%94+never+a+gasp+to+camera.%29%0AHERO+%E2%80%94+MOTIVE%3A+she+taught+him+the+carnival%27s+game+%E2%80%94+vanish+behind+the+moving+curtain%2C+reappear+where+you+are+not+expected.+GOAL%3A+arrive+%E2%80%94+not+to+catch+her%3A+to+be+there%3B+the+first+touch+of+their+story+is+HIS+to+make.+TACTIC%3A+he+stays+out+of+sight+while+she+is+hunting%2C+and+comes+up+quietly+through+the+standing+crowd+behind+her+only+once+she+has+stopped+looking+%E2%80%94+and+his+bare+LEFT+hand+reaches+DOWN+toward+her+bare+RIGHT+hand+resting+at+her+side%3A+his+INDEX+FINGER+leading%2C+extended+ahead+of+the+other+fingers%2C+the+others+relaxed+and+softly+curled+%E2%80%94+and+it+is+the+PAD+OF+HIS+INDEX+FINGER+that+makes+the+touch%2C+gently+grazing+her+fingers%3A+soft%2C+warm%2C+certain+%E2%80%94+skin+to+skin+%E2%80%94+an+arrival%3B+%22I+am+here.%22+%28Safety%3A+the+touch+gentle%2C+never+forceful%3B+the+reach+unhurried.%29%0ACROWD+%E2%80%94+one+task+and+one+only%3A+WATCH+THE+SHOW+IN+THE+MIDDLE+OF+THE+SQUARE+%E2%80%94+standing+still%2C+absorbed%2C+backs+to+the+camera%2C+cheering+and+applauding+toward+the+centre.+Nobody+looks+back%2C+nobody+notices+the+GIRL+passing%2C+nobody+moves+from+their+place.+%28Safety%3A+alive+stillness+%E2%80%
